- **nanobrain-agents-tools**: How to author Agents and Tools in nanobrain, including LLM model selection, system-prompt-in-YAML rule, Tool subclassing (`async def execute`), tool registration via config (NOT programmatic), the A2A (Agent-to-Agent) protocol surface, and the MCP (Model Context Protocol) client integration. Read whenever you create an Agent, a Tool, or an external-system integration. ([AlexandrNP/apecx-mcp-integration](https://github.com/AlexandrNP/apecx-mcp-integration/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/nanobrain-agents-tools) · ★4)
- **nanobrain-config-yaml**: How to write YAML configuration files for nanobrain components. Covers the `class:` + `config:` pattern for nested references, environment variable interpolation, the file-path resolution order, the difference between `ConfigBase` (file-only) and inline-dict-tolerant configs (DataUnit/Link/Trigger), and the standard fields shared across all components. Read this whenever you create or edit a `*.yml` under a nanobrain project. ([AlexandrNP/apecx-mcp-integration](https://github.com/AlexandrNP/apecx-mcp-integration/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/nanobrain-config-yaml) · ★4)
- **nanobrain-data-units-triggers-links**: The event-driven contract — how DataUnits emit change events, how Triggers subscribe (or poll), and how Links transfer data from one step's output to another step's input. Read whenever you wire steps together, define triggers, or add a link. Covers all DataUnit subclasses (Memory/File/String/Stream/object-ref), all Trigger types (DataUnitChange/AllDataReceived/Timer/Manual), all Link types (Direct/Queue/Transform/Conditional/File/Academy), the AsyncTriggerExecutor's deadlock prevention, and the verbatim error messages. ([AlexandrNP/apecx-mcp-integration](https://github.com/AlexandrNP/apecx-mcp-integration/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/nanobrain-data-units-triggers-links) · ★4)
- **nanobrain-executors**: Choosing and configuring an Executor (Local, Thread, Process, Parsl, GlobusCompute). Covers the async contract (`process()` must be async), executor-specific deadlock risks, the Parsl/HPC code path (Aurora PBS, ProxyStore, dynamic scaling), the Globus Compute remote-endpoint path (dispatch a step to Aurora via Globus Compute + Globus Auth), per-step `executor_config:` binding, and verbatim error messages. Read whenever you set or change a step's `executor:` field, especially before declaring distributed execution "working". ([AlexandrNP/apecx-mcp-integration](https://github.com/AlexandrNP/apecx-mcp-integration/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/nanobrain-executors) · ★4)
- **nanobrain-from-config**: How to create nanobrain components correctly via the mandatory `from_config` pattern. Read this FIRST before authoring any nanobrain code (steps, workflows, agents, tools, data units, links, triggers, executors). Covers the call sequence inside `from_config()`, configuration file path resolution, the four supported input shapes (str/Path/dict/Pydantic), the `class:` auto-delegation trick, the `_allow_direct_instantiation` backdoor, and the verbatim error messages the framework raises on violations. ([AlexandrNP/apecx-mcp-integration](https://github.com/AlexandrNP/apecx-mcp-integration/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/nanobrain-from-config) · ★4)
- **nanobrain-lightweight**: When and how to use the lightweight nanobrain authoring path (`nanobrain.lightweight.WorkflowBuilder` + auto-discovery) instead of hand-writing YAML or driving the apecx-mcp composer.
